## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the supplied repository and data assets, especially capital.csv, and report the relevant columns, time coverage, missingness, and how the hundred-year bins and PCA appear to be defined or implemented. Then propose a concise analysis approach to determine the symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 values and their distribution along PC2. Flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties (including PCA sign conventions and whether the question concerns loadings or scores). Do not yet perform the full analysis or state the findings.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv as archaeological capital data and supplies context that symbolic capital comprises hatchets/swords, daggers, and house size, with analysis in 100-year bins. PCA implementation details and whether the requested component values refer to loadings or observations remain unspecified.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository/data and identify PCA/bins workflow; Clarify/flag consequential ambiguities and propose analysis; findings pending

**Agent reply (plan)**

Repository inspection found only the three staged CSVs, task metadata, and no analysis scripts/source code documenting the actual PCA or hundred-year binning. `capital.csv` has 39 rows, 15 columns: BCE, group/color, and 12 Z-valued indicators. BCE covers 500–4300 BCE in 100-year increments (39 unique values; file order descends). Columns corresponding to the symbolic-capital examples are `Zhausgr` (house size), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords); other indicator columns include `ZCU_AU`, `Zamber`, `ZMonument`, `ZHort`, `ZSichel`, `ZBeil`, `ZMW`, `ZKeform`, and `Zkeverz`. Dataset metadata says group/color are dropped after loading. The raw fields are read as strings and numeric values use decimal commas; blank-value missingness is not yet validated as numeric after parsing. Raw pandas NA counts show zero missing cells, but that alone does not rule out nonnumeric or sentinel missing values.

Proposed next step after approval: parse decimal commas to numeric, audit conversion failures and missingness, and verify unique chronological spacing. Use BCE to define hundred-year slices (the rows already appear to be one observation per century), then inspect any available workflow/context in task metadata and clarify whether PCA is global across all slices or independently refit within each slice. If no PCA implementation is supplied, test a transparent reconstruction from the 12 indicator columns and report its scaling/centering choice and explained variance, while clearly separating that reconstruction from the potentially intended PCA. Summarize symbolic variables’ PC1 loadings (variable characterization) and PC2 distribution, and optionally scores by time bin (observation characterization), explicitly labeling which is which. Keep any PCA sign convention caveat: component signs are mathematically arbitrary and must be oriented consistently or discussed invariantly. Also flag that the question may use “values” to mean loadings, component scores, or plotted positions; “distributed across PC2” could mean where these variables lie in a loading plot versus temporal observations’ scores.

**Shared evidence:**

Files present: data/capital.csv, data/time_series_data.csv, data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv, task.json; no source code found by repository search. capital.csv inspection: shape (39,15), columns BCE/group/color plus 12 listed Z fields; BCE min 500 max 4300, 39 unique values, descending in file, apparent interval 100 years. Raw NA count was zero in every column, though numeric parsing/placeholder audit is still needed. Metadata documents Zhausgr=house size, ZDolch=daggers, ZAxtSchwert=hatchets/swords and says group/color dropped after loading.

**Assumptions:** The user's request at this stage is limited to repository/data inspection and an approach, not the substantive PCA findings.

**Uncertainties:** No PCA code or explanation of binning was found among staged repository files.; The intended PCA may be on all 12 Z variables globally or independently for each hundred-year time slice.; Question wording does not establish whether PC1 'values' refers to variable loadings or row scores, nor precisely what PC2 distribution refers to.; PCA signs are arbitrary; reproducing a sign without original implementation would be conventional rather than uniquely meaningful.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Thanks, this inspection is sufficient to set the analysis direction. Proceed with the main work: parse and validate the numeric fields, then inspect the symbolic-capital variables’ PCA characterization, distinguishing variable loadings from time-slice scores. Because no original workflow is available and the question most naturally asks how the variables themselves relate to PC1 and PC2, prioritize a loading-based reconstruction across the 12 capital indicators using all century rows; explicitly state centering/scaling and any other PCA choices. If metadata or data patterns materially indicate a different PCA design, flag that rather than silently substituting it. Report PC1 loadings/signs and how the three variables are positioned or spread on PC2, with a concise, data-grounded hypothesis. You may mention temporal score context only if needed to disambiguate. Treat component sign orientation as conventional, and do not claim this reproduces an undocumented original analysis.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** Inspection reports 39 century observations and the three named symbolic-capital columns, but no source PCA or binning implementation. The approach addresses the question’s likely variable-loading focus while retaining the reported ambiguity and sign indeterminacy as caveats.

**Open objectives:** Validate numeric parsing and missingness in capital.csv.; Reconstruct and report PCA variable loadings for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords, especially PC1 and their PC2 positions/distribution.; Provide a concise hypothesis with reproducible PCA choices and uncertainty caveats.

**Agent reply (result)**

**Data-grounded hypothesis:** Across the 39 century observations from 4300 to 500 BCE, house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) all have positive, moderate PC1 loadings in a reconstruction using all 12 capital indicators. With PC1 oriented so house size is positive, their PC1 coefficients are about 0.23, 0.28, and 0.30, respectively. On PC2, the three loadings are all positive under the stated orientation, but not equally distributed: house size lies appreciably farther along PC2 (0.36) than daggers (0.12) and hatchets/swords (0.067), which sit closer to the PC2 origin. Thus the three cluster more closely in their PC1 contribution than in their PC2 characterization.

**Reconstruction details and caveats:** Parsed decimal commas to numeric values. All 39 × 13 numeric fields (BCE plus 12 indicators) parsed with zero missing values or conversion failures; BCE contains 39 unique values, descending from 4300 to 500 in exact 100-year steps. I excluded BCE, group, and color from the PCA and used the 12 indicator columns. They are supplied as Z-valued measures and are nearly unit-SD for most fields; I centered them but did not standardize/rescale again. PCA was computed from the sample covariance matrix. PC1 and PC2 explain approximately 31.8% and 20.4% of variance (52.3% combined). The displayed values are eigenvector coefficients (“component coefficients”; often informally called loadings), not scores or correlation-scaled loadings. Signs are conventional: PC1 was oriented to make house size positive, and PC2 to make daggers positive; flipping either component flips all corresponding signs without changing the PCA.

This is a transparent loading-based reconstruction, not a claim to recover an undocumented original PCA. The hundred-year sampling structure supports treating the rows as successive time slices; there is no supplied code indicating that PCA was separately refit within each slice, which would not be estimable from one row per slice. Another loading convention (e.g., eigenvectors multiplied by square roots of eigenvalues) or rescaling may change numerical values. The wording could instead refer to scores for time slices; scores are distinct observations in PC space and vary over time, so they are not the variable loadings summarized above.

**Shared evidence:**

Execution diagnostics: 39 rows; BCE unique values 500–4300, all 100 years apart; every numeric conversion succeeded and all parsed numeric fields were complete. Using the 12 indicators, PC1/PC2 explained variance ratios were 0.3184/0.2042. Symbolic eigenvector coefficients (PC1, PC2): house size (0.2283, 0.3644), daggers (0.2793, 0.1195), hatchets/swords (0.3010, 0.0666). The full execution output also gave all indicator coefficients and showed most indicator SDs near 1, except sickles (SD 0.538), supporting the stated choice not to rescale.

**Assumptions:** All 12 non-temporal Z-valued indicators are the capital variables intended for the PCA; BCE is a time index, not an indicator.; Because the question asks how variables are characterized, “values on PC1” is interpreted primarily as component coefficients/loadings rather than time-slice scores.; The supplied Z measures were intended to be used without further rescaling; this reconstruction centers but does not standardize them again.; Rows are successive century slices, and the requested loading summary is from a single PCA across all rows rather than separate per-century PCAs.

**Uncertainties:** No original code or methods description specifies PCA scaling, component/loading convention, or its sign orientation.; One field (`ZSichel`) has SD 0.538 while the other indicators are mostly near SD 1. Different scaling could affect the reconstructed components.; Question phrasing could refer to time-slice scores or a plot convention; the submitted interpretation makes the loadings-vs-scores distinction explicit.

Their PC2 coefficients are also positive after orienting PC2 toward daggers, but house size (0.364) is farther along PC2 than daggers (0.120) and hatchets/swords (0.067), which are near the PC2 origin. ### 3. Researcher — conclude

The reported reconstruction answers the question: the three symbolic-capital variables have positive, moderate PC1 coefficients, while house size has a distinctly larger PC2 coefficient than daggers and hatchets/swords. These are coefficients from an explicitly described reconstruction, not verified values from an undocumented original PCA; component signs are arbitrary.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The agent reports successful numeric validation and a centered covariance PCA of the 12 indicators, with PC1/PC2 variance explained of 31.8%/20.4%. Reported coefficients distinguish PC1 similarity from PC2 separation and state scaling/sign conventions and the loading-versus-score interpretation.

**Open objectives:** None

  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Parse numeric capital columns and reconstruct full-row PCA loadings\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/analyze.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nprint('shape',df.shape)\\nprint('columns',df.columns.tolist())\\n# Parse decimal comma in all value columns, and report failures\\nnumcols=[c for c in df if c not in ['group','color']]\\nraw=df[numcols].copy()\\nfor c in numcols:\\n    df[c]=pd.to_numeric(df[c].str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')\\nfor c in numcols:\\n    bad=raw[c].notna() & df[c].isna()\\n    print(f'{c}: missing={df[c].isna().sum()} conversion_failures={bad.sum()} min={df[c].min()} max={df[c].max()}')\\nprint('BCE sorted descending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_decreasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'steps',sorted(df.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique()))\\nprint('All columns complete numeric',df[numcols].notna().all().all())\\nvars=[c for c in numcols if c!='BCE']\\nX=df[vars]\\nprint('PCA indicators',len(vars),vars)\\nprint('mean/std summary:\\\\n',X.agg(['mean','std']).T.to_string())\\n# PCA of raw already-Z-valued indicator variables, centered by PCA, no additional standardization\\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\\nsc=pca.fit_transform(X)\\ncomponents=pca.components_.copy()\\n# orient PC1 so HouseSize loading positive; orient PC2 so dagger loading positive\\nif components[0,vars.index('Zhausgr')]<0:\\n    components[0]*=-1; sc[:,0]*=-1\\nif components[1,vars.index('ZDolch')]<0:\\n    components[1]*=-1; sc[:,1]*=-1\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_,'eigenvalues',pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('loadings (component eigenvector coeffs):')\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=vars.index(c); On PC2, the three loadings are all positive under the stated orientation, but not equally distributed: house size lies appreciably farther along PC2 (0.36) than daggers (0.12) and hatchets/swords (0.067), which sit closer to the PC2 origin. I excluded BCE, group, and color from the PCA and used the 12 indicator columns. They are supplied as Z-valued measures and are nearly unit-SD for most fields; I centered them but did not standardize/rescale again. PCA was computed from the sample covariance matrix. PC1 and PC2 explain approximately 31.8% and 20.4% of variance (52.3% combined). The displayed values are eigenvector coefficients (\\u201ccomponent coefficients\\u201d; often informally called loadings), not scores or correlation-scaled loadings. Signs are conventional: PC1 was oriented to make house size positive, and PC2 to make daggers positive; flipping either component flips all corresponding signs without changing the PCA.\\n\\nThis is a transparent loading-based reconstruction, not a claim to recover an undocumented original PCA. The hundred-year sampling structure supports treating the rows as successive time slices; there is no supplied code indicating that PCA was separately refit within each slice, which would not be estimable from one row per slice. Another loading convention (e.g., eigenvectors multiplied by square roots of eigenvalues) or rescaling may change numerical values. Their PC2 coefficients are also positive after orienting PC2 toward daggers, but house size (0.364) is farther along PC2 than daggers (0.120) and hatchets/swords (0.067), which are near the PC2 origin.   "command": "cat > /tmp/analyze.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\np='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\nprint('shape',df.shape)\nprint('columns',df.columns.tolist())\n# Parse decimal comma in all value columns, and report failures\nnumcols=[c for c in df if c not in ['group','color']]\nraw=df[numcols].copy()\nfor c in numcols:\n    df[c]=pd.to_numeric(df[c].str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')\nfor c in numcols:\n    bad=raw[c].notna() & df[c].isna()\n    print(f'{c}: missing={df[c].isna().sum()} conversion_failures={bad.sum()} min={df[c].min()} max={df[c].max()}')\nprint('BCE sorted descending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_decreasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'steps',sorted(df.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique()))\nprint('All columns complete numeric',df[numcols].notna().all().all())\nvars=[c for c in numcols if c!='BCE']\nX=df[vars]\nprint('PCA indicators',len(vars),vars)\nprint('mean/std summary:\\n',X.agg(['mean','std']).T.to_string())\n# PCA of raw already-Z-valued indicator variables, centered by PCA, no additional standardization\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\nsc=pca.fit_transform(X)\ncomponents=pca.components_.copy()\n# orient PC1 so HouseSize loading positive; orient PC2 so dagger loading positive\nif components[0,vars.index('Zhausgr')]<0:\n    components[0]*=-1; sc[:,0]*=-1\nif components[1,vars.index('ZDolch')]<0:\n    components[1]*=-1; sc[:,1]*=-1\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_,'eigenvalues',pca.explained_variance_)\nprint('loadings (component eigenvector coeffs):')\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n    i=vars.index(c); print(c,'PC1',components[0,i],'PC2',components[1,i], 'score correlation',np.corrcoef(X[c],sc[:,0])[0,1],np.corrcoef(X[c],sc[:,1])[0,1])\nprint('all loadings:\\n',pd.DataFrame(components.T,index=vars,columns=['PC1','PC2']).sort_values('PC1',ascending=False).to_string())\nprint('PC2 symbolic loading range',components[1,[vars.index(c) for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]].min(),components[1,[vars.index(c) for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]].max())\n# Show time score distribution by CE bins\nscores=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':sc[:,0],'PC2_score':sc[:,1]})\nprint('score summaries all',scores[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].agg(['min','median','max']).to_dict())\nprint('score earliest/latest rows:\\n',scores.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).head(3).to_string(index=False),'\\n',scores.sort_values('BCE').head(3).to_string(index=False))\n# correlation matrices\nprint('indicators correlation with symbolic vars:\\n',X[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].corr().to_string())\nPY\npython /tmp/analyze.py",

  "answer": "**Data-grounded hypothesis:** Across the 39 century observations from 4300 to 500 BCE, house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) all have positive, moderate PC1 loadings in a reconstruction using all 12 capital indicators. With PC1 oriented so house size is positive, their PC1 coefficients are about 0.23, 0.28, and 0.30, respectively. On PC2, the three loadings are all positive under the stated orientation, but not equally distributed: house size lies appreciably farther along PC2 (0.36) than daggers (0.12) and hatchets/swords (0.067), which sit closer to the PC2 origin. Thus the three cluster more closely in their PC1 contribution than in their PC2 characterization.\n\n**Reconstruction details and caveats:** Parsed decimal commas to numeric values. All 39 × 13 numeric fields (BCE plus 12 indicators) parsed with zero missing values or conversion failures; BCE contains 39 unique values, descending from 4300 to 500 in exact 100-year steps. I excluded BCE, group, and color from the PCA and used the 12 indicator columns. They are supplied as Z-valued measures and are nearly unit-SD for most fields; I centered them but did not standardize/rescale again. PCA was computed from the sample covariance matrix. PC1 and PC2 explain approximately 31.8% and 20.4% of variance (52.3% combined). The displayed values are eigenvector coefficients (“component coefficients”; often informally called loadings), not scores or correlation-scaled loadings. Signs are conventional: PC1 was oriented to make house size positive, and PC2 to make daggers positive; flipping either component flips all corresponding signs without changing the PCA.\n\nThis is a transparent loading-based reconstruction, not a claim to recover an undocumented original PCA. The hundred-year sampling structure supports treating the rows as successive time slices; there is no supplied code indicating that PCA was separately refit within each slice, which would not be estimable from one row per slice. Another loading convention (e.g., eigenvectors multiplied by square roots of eigenvalues) or rescaling may change numerical values. The wording could instead refer to scores for time slices; scores are distinct observations in PC space and vary over time, so they are not the variable loadings summarized above.",

You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.         "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Parse numeric capital columns and reconstruct full-row PCA loadings\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/analyze.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nprint('shape',df.shape)\\nprint('columns',df.columns.tolist())\\n# Parse decimal comma in all value columns, and report failures\\nnumcols=[c for c in df if c not in ['group','color']]\\nraw=df[numcols].copy()\\nfor c in numcols:\\n    df[c]=pd.to_numeric(df[c].str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')\\nfor c in numcols:\\n    bad=raw[c].notna() & df[c].isna()\\n    print(f'{c}: missing={df[c].isna().sum()} conversion_failures={bad.sum()} min={df[c].min()} max={df[c].max()}')\\nprint('BCE sorted descending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_decreasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'steps',sorted(df.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique()))\\nprint('All columns complete numeric',df[numcols].notna().all().all())\\nvars=[c for c in numcols if c!='BCE']\\nX=df[vars]\\nprint('PCA indicators',len(vars),vars)\\nprint('mean/std summary:\\\\n',X.agg(['mean','std']).T.to_string())\\n# PCA of raw already-Z-valued indicator variables, centered by PCA, no additional standardization\\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\\nsc=pca.fit_transform(X)\\ncomponents=pca.components_.copy()\\n# orient PC1 so HouseSize loading positive; orient PC2 so dagger loading positive\\nif components[0,vars.index('Zhausgr')]<0:\\n    components[0]*=-1; sc[:,0]*=-1\\nif components[1,vars.index('ZDolch')]<0:\\n    components[1]*=-1; sc[:,1]*=-1\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_,'eigenvalues',pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('loadings (component eigenvector coeffs):')\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=vars.index(c); print(c,'PC1',components[0,i],'PC2',components[1,i], 'score correlation',np.corrcoef(X[c],sc[:,0])[0,1],np.corrcoef(X[c],sc[:,1])[0,1])\\nprint('all loadings:\\\\n',pd.DataFrame(components.T,index=vars,columns=['PC1','PC2']).sort_values('PC1',ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 symbolic loading range',components[1,[vars.index(c) for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]].min(),components[1,[vars.index(c) for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]].max())\\n# Show time score distribution by CE bins\\nscores=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':sc[:,0],'PC2_score':sc[:,1]})\\nprint('score summaries all',scores[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].agg(['min','median','max']).to_dict())\\nprint('score earliest/latest rows:\\\\n',scores.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).head(3).to_string(index=False),'\\\\n',scores.sort_values('BCE').head(3).to_string(index=False))\\n# correlation matrices\\nprint('indicators correlation with symbolic vars:\\\\n',X[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].corr().to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/analyze.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"
      }
    }
  ]
}

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Parse numeric capital columns and reconstruct full-row PCA loadings\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/analyze.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p,dtype=str)\\nprint('shape',df.shape)\\nprint('columns',df.columns.tolist())\\n# Parse decimal comma in all value columns, and report failures\\nnumcols=[c for c in df if c not in ['group','color']]\\nraw=df[numcols].copy()\\nfor c in numcols:\\n    df[c]=pd.to_numeric(df[c].str.strip().str.replace(',','.',regex=False),errors='coerce')\\nfor c in numcols:\\n    bad=raw[c].notna() & df[c].isna()\\n    print(f'{c}: missing={df[c].isna().sum()} conversion_failures={bad.sum()} min={df[c].min()} max={df[c].max()}')\\nprint('BCE sorted descending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_decreasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'steps',sorted(df.BCE.diff().dropna().abs().unique()))\\nprint('All columns complete numeric',df[numcols].notna().all().all())\\nvars=[c for c in numcols if c!='BCE']\\nX=df[vars]\\nprint('PCA indicators',len(vars),vars)\\nprint('mean/std summary:\\\\n',X.agg(['mean','std']).T.to_string())\\n# PCA of raw already-Z-valued indicator variables, centered by PCA, no additional standardization\\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\\nsc=pca.fit_transform(X)\\ncomponents=pca.components_.copy()\\n# orient PC1 so HouseSize loading positive; orient PC2 so dagger loading positive\\nif components[0,vars.index('Zhausgr')]<0:\\n    components[0]*=-1; sc[:,0]*=-1\\nif components[1,vars.index('ZDolch')]<0:\\n    components[1]*=-1; sc[:,1]*=-1\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_,'eigenvalues',pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('loadings (component eigenvector coeffs):')\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=vars.index(c); print(c,'PC1',components[0,i],'PC2',components[1,i], 'score correlation',np.corrcoef(X[c],sc[:,0])[0,1],np.corrcoef(X[c],sc[:,1])[0,1])\\nprint('all loadings:\\\\n',pd.DataFrame(components.T,index=vars,columns=['PC1','PC2']).sort_values('PC1',ascending=False).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 symbolic loading range',components[1,[vars.index(c) for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]].min(),components[1,[vars.index(c) for c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']]].max())\\n# Show time score distribution by CE bins\\nscores=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':sc[:,0],'PC2_score':sc[:,1]})\\nprint('score summaries all',scores[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].agg(['min','median','max']).to_dict())\\nprint('score earliest/latest rows:\\\\n',scores.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).head(3).to_string(index=False),'\\\\n',scores.sort_values('BCE').head(3).to_string(index=False))\\n# correlation matrices\\nprint('indicators correlation with symbolic vars:\\\\n',X[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].corr().to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/analyze.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

orient PC2 so dagger loading positive\\nif components[0,vars.index('Zhausgr')]<0:\\n    components[0]*=-1; sc[:,0]*=-1\\nif components[1,vars.index('ZDolch')]<0:\\n    components[1]*=-1; sc[:,1]*=-1\\nprint('explained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_,'eigenvalues',pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('loadings (component eigenvector coeffs):')\\nfor c in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=vars.index(c);
